limited to the sectionalizing of troubles to defective
units which must be replaced as a unit, or to the               sequence as the operator follows when
localizing of defective parts for which running                 initially applying power to the equip-
spares are available. An equipment performance                  ment. The simplest procedure when us-
checklist (table IIT) is included primarily as a                ing the checklist is to perform the various
sectionalization aid. Localizing procedures for                  items on the list in sequence until an ab-
the various units are outlined in paragraphs 81                  normal indication is observed. When the
through 86.                                                      operator has become familiar with the

80. Equipment Performance Checklist                             the list to his own advantage, omitting

list provides a detailed logical procedure for check-            check.
ing the operation of the radio set. It should be
practiced after the initial installation of the equip-           The first five columns of the checklist
ment before the equipment is placed in service,                  show the items to be checked, the units
periodically thereafter, and as an aid in trouble                which are involved, the action to be taken,
shooting.                                                        and the normal indication to be expected.

     Section Il. DEMOLITION OF MATERIEL TO PREVENT ENEMY USE

108. General                                           b. Cut. Cut cords, cables, headsets, and wiring,
                                                    using axes, handaxes, machetes, or bayonets.
   The demolition procedures outlined in para-
graph 109 will be used to prevent the enemy from       c. Burn. Burn cords, cables, resistors, capaci-
using or salvaging this equipment. Demolition       tors, coils, wiring, and technical manuals, using
of the equipment will be accomplished only upon     gasoline, kerosene, oil, flame throwers, or incen-
order of the commander,                             diary grenades.

109. Methods of Destruction                            d. Bend. Bend panels, cases, chassis, mounting,
                                                    and antenna mast sections.
   a. Smash. Smash the crystals, controls, tubes,
coils, switches, capacitors, transformers, micro-      e. Haplosives. If explosives are necessary, use
phones, and headsets, using sledges, axes, hand-    firearms, grenades, or TNT.
axes, pickaxes, hammers, crowbars, or heavy tools.
                                                       f. Disposal. Bury or scatter the destroyed parts
                                                    in slit trenches, fox holes, or other holes, or throw
                                                    them into streams.

                                                       g. Destroy Everything.
